# NUMBERS

Every number the paper may use. Labels: **bound** (exact inequality evaluated on data), **reference** (attainable forecaster using the true dynamics), **learned** (trained model), **estimate** (Monte Carlo population quantity with interval). Horizons are restricted means E[min(λT, 27)] in Lyapunov times; primary score = future frames, ε = 0.3. Readings are the frozen margins only (FREEZE.md 2.8).

Freeze commit: `f750ca1`. Generated by `scripts/make_numbers.py` at SHA `100b1597e175f81284be2faf5d5bf08b505ef6f3-dirty`.

## P. POST-FREEZE robustness: k-means random_state 0-4 at 4 and 6 bits (frozen-seed codebook primary)

Source `results/postfreeze/codebook_seeds_spread.csv` · SHA `424c7ed903113826fdf49ed3edb67961138fa959`

## P2. POST-FREEZE: exchange-law predictions by per-state h and by geometric-mean distortion, next to the frozen RMS prediction (criterion outcomes stay as frozen)

Source `results/postfreeze/exchange_law_variants.csv` · SHA `100b1597e175f81284be2faf5d5bf08b505ef6f3-dirty`

Post-freeze, not pre-registered. meas = **reference**; predictions = **estimate**. The frozen criterion outcomes are unchanged; the tolerance column is informational. Per-state errors outside the calibrated h range are clamped to its end (clamped share shown next to every prediction), which biases those predictions upward; cells with more than 25% clamped are marked UNINFORMATIVE.

## P6. POST-FREEZE: slope x D_eff and implied r = ln2/(slope x D_eff), beside local r at codebook scales (section R)

Source `results/postfreeze/slope_x_deff.csv` · SHA `100b1597e175f81284be2faf5d5bf08b505ef6f3-dirty`

Post-freeze **estimate**. Intervals propagate the slope bootstrap (panel states) and the D_eff bootstrap (calibration trajectories) jointly. Fits: all rates 4-12; the frozen even rates {6,8,10,12}; all rates 6-12; all rates 8-12. The R8-12 rows carry the paired difference of implied r against the R6-12 fit (settling check; shared bootstrap replicates). Local r: fresh orientation, central differences at h-grid points between the 12-bit and 4-bit calibration distortions. l96_10 reaches below the 0.3 sigma_A tolerance only at 12 bits and l96_20 never within 4-12 bits, so their slopes and implied r are not meaningful as exchange rates.

## K2B. POST-FREEZE EXTENSION K2: E5.2 ties and bound survival under Amendment 2 B4 (4 bits)

Source `results/ext/e5_tie_baselines.csv` · SHA `100b1597e175f81284be2faf5d5bf08b505ef6f3-dirty`

Tie = P(VPT = T_out | Delta < T_out <= W, failure observed) (Amendment 2 B4). Automatic first-frame cases (T_out = Delta) and jointly censored cases are reported separately and are not ties. S_out(tau) = P(lambda T_out > tau), censored states survive. Superseded tables are kept as results/ext/e5_tie_baselines_SUPERSEDED_pre_amendment{1,2}.csv (not in NUMBERS).

## M. Single-frame decomposition, lorenz28 (Amendment 1; 150,000 calibration states)

Source `results/decomposition/decomposition.csv` · SHA `424c7ed903113826fdf49ed3edb67961138fa959`

T_info is a **bound** (single-frame information bound on ensemble RMSE); T_codebook, T_projected_DI and the shares are **estimate**. Known-zero test: original threshold 1e-10 fails at 4 bits; amended threshold 1e-6 (FREEZE.md Amendment 1) passes at every rate.
